    <itunes:title>Cornell&#39;s Chris Anderson: Why Hotels Can&#39;t Wait on AI</itunes:title>
    <title>Cornell&#39;s Chris Anderson: Why Hotels Can&#39;t Wait on AI</title>
    <itunes:summary><![CDATA[In this episode, Cornell professor Chris Anderson shares why hotels cannot afford to treat AI as a "wait-and-see" issue. Drawing on research he conducted about how different traveler segments use AI, he explains why hotel discovery is becoming more contextual and why reviews and trusted third-party information matter. We also discuss what hoteliers can test now, why integrated technology will matter more, and how more specific guest requests could eventually change what hotels offer. Chr...]]></itunes:summary>
    <description><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://business.cornell.edu/profiles/cka9/'>Cornell professor</a> <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/chriskanderson/'>Chris Anderson</a> shares why hotels cannot afford to treat AI as a &quot;wait-and-see&quot; issue. Drawing on <a href='https://ecommons.cornell.edu/entities/publication/6fddf25c-ace1-4991-8b22-cfacc658eec9'>research he conducted</a> about how different traveler segments use AI, he explains why hotel discovery is becoming more contextual and why reviews and trusted third-party information matter. We also discuss what hoteliers can test now, why integrated technology will matter more, and how more specific guest requests could eventually change what hotels offer.</p><p>Chris Anderson will be speaking at Destination AI 2026. <a href='https://destinationaiforum.com/'>Learn more about the event here</a>.</p> <p>A few more resources:</p><ul><li>If you&apos;re new to Hospitality Daily, <a href='https://podcast.hospitalitydaily.com/p/start-here/'>start here</a>. </li><li>You can <a href='https://podcast.hospitalitydaily.com/contact/'>send me a message here</a> with questions, comments, or guest suggestions</li><li>If you want to get my summary and actionable insights from each episode delivered to your inbox each day, <a href='https://www.hospitalitydaily.com/subscribe'>subscribe here for free</a>.</li><li>Follow Hospitality Daily and join the conversation on <a href='https://www.youtube.com/@hospitalitydaily'>YouTube</a>, <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/company/hospitalitydaily/'>LinkedIn</a>, and <a href='https://www.instagram.com/hospitality_daily/'>Instagram</a>.</li><li>If you want to advertise on Hospitality Daily, <a href='https://www.passionfroot.me/hospitalitydaily'>here are the ways we can work together</a>.</li></ul><p>If you found this episode interesting or helpful, send it to someone on your team so you can turn the ideas into action and benefit your business and the people you serve!</p><p><em>Music for this show is produced by Clay Bassford of </em><a href='https://www.bespokesound.co/'><em>Bespoke Sound: Music Identity Design for Hospitality Brands</em></a></p>]]></description>
    <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://business.cornell.edu/profiles/cka9/'>Cornell professor</a> <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/chriskanderson/'>Chris Anderson</a> shares why hotels cannot afford to treat AI as a &quot;wait-and-see&quot; issue. Drawing on <a href='https://ecommons.cornell.edu/entities/publication/6fddf25c-ace1-4991-8b22-cfacc658eec9'>research he conducted</a> about how different traveler segments use AI, he explains why hotel discovery is becoming more contextual and why reviews and trusted third-party information matter.     <description><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/emilygoretskyjohnson/'><b>Emily Johnson</b></a>, founder of <a href='https://www.elevatehospitalitycollective.com/about'>Elevate Hospitality Collective;</a> <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/erica-huss-alltoowell/'><b>Erica Huss</b></a>, creator of <a href='https://www.ericahuss.com/about'>All Too Well;</a> and <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/jenveralle/'><b>Jen Veralle</b></a>, founder of <a href='https://www.jenveralle.com/'>Mindful Drinking Movement</a> and <a href='https://www.saunaandsobriety.com/'>Sauna and Sobriety</a>, share a practical approach to hotel wellness that doesn&apos;t start with a big CapEx budget.</p><p>They explore why the guest room may be the most powerful wellness facility on a property, how a thoughtful non-alcoholic beverage program can create incremental revenue, and how guided sauna and community experiences can give guests restorative ways to connect without depending on alcohol.</p><p>This is a practical look at building wellness into the complete guest journey using rooms, F&amp;B programs, existing spaces, and the knowledge already present within hotel teams.     <description><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/christinapoon/'>Christina Poon</a>, the General Manager at the <a href='https://www.editionhotels.com/'>Detroit EDITION</a>, shares how she&apos;s building a great hotel guest experience before the property opens.     <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/christinapoon/'>Christina Poon</a>, the General Manager at the <a href='https://www.editionhotels.com/'>Detroit EDITION</a>, shares how she&apos;s building a great hotel guest experience before the property opens.     <title>The PMS Wars: AI Changes the Interface, Not the Foundation - Martin Soler</title>
    <itunes:summary><![CDATA[Martin Soler returns to explore "The PMS Wars," his ongoing series examining one of the most important shifts in hotel technology.     <description><![CDATA[<p>Martin Soler returns to explore &quot;The PMS Wars,&quot; his ongoing series examining one of the most important shifts in hotel technology. What started as a race from legacy systems to cloud-based PMS platforms is now evolving into a conversation about AI, data infrastructure, and the future of hotel operations.</p><p>In this episode, Martin explains why hospitality adopts technology differently from other industries, why changing a PMS remains one of the hardest decisions a hotel can make, and why AI will depend on strong data foundations rather than replace them. He also shares his perspective on how AI could reshape software interfaces while making the underlying PMS more important than ever.</p><p>Referenced Articles:<br/>• <a href='https://martinsoler.substack.com/p/133-ai-and-the-pms-wars'>AI and the PMS Wars, Part I</a><br/>• <a href='https://martinsoler.substack.com/p/155-the-pms-wars-and-ai'>The PMS Wars and AI, Part II </a></p><p>Key Topics:<br/>• Why cloud PMS adoption took longer than expected<br/>• The challenges of PMS migration and vendor consolidation<br/>• Why hotel technology evolves differently from other industries<br/>• The role of APIs and structured data in the AI era<br/>• How AI may change hotel software interfaces<br/>• Why PMS platforms remain the foundation of hotel operations</p> <p>A What started as a race from legacy systems to cloud-based PMS platforms is now evolving into a conversation about AI, data infrastructure, and the future of hotel operations.</p><p>In this episode, Martin explains why hospitality adopts technology differently from other industries, why changing a PMS remains one of the hardest decisions a hotel can make, and why AI will depend on strong data foundations rather than replace them. He also shares his perspective on how AI could reshape software interfaces while making the underlying PMS more important than ever.</p><p>Referenced Articles:<br/>• <a     <description><![CDATA[<p><em>This episode is sponsored by Actabl. </em><a href='https://actabl.com/news/actabl-introduces-altitude/'><em>Learn more about its new product, Altitude, here</em></a><em>.</em> </p><p>For years now, AI has promised hotel leaders something it hasn&apos;t delivered: the ability to ask a question and get an answer you can actually trust. Today, that changes with the launch of Actabl Altitude.</p><p>In this episode, I sit down with <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/stephen-german-88aa8b51/'>Stephen German</a>, Actabl&apos;s SVP of Product, to tell the story behind it. We get into why most AI answers fall apart the moment you check them, what it really takes to trust a number enough to hand it to your CFO, and why the foundation under the AI matters more than the AI itself.</p><p>Stephen makes a distinction that reframes the whole conversation. Most AI is probabilistic, so ask the same question twice, and you can get two different answers. When you&apos;re dealing with forecasts and P&amp;Ls, you need deterministic results, the same right answer every time, with logic underneath that knows the difference between your primary forecast and your locked one. That&apos;s the line between an interesting demo and a tool you can run a business on.</p><p>We also talk about who this is really for. Above-property leaders, the regional VPs and COOs, have been underserved by hotel tech for a long time. Altitude lets them have a conversation with their data, follow the thread at the speed of thought, and dig into a problem without waiting days for three different teams to pull reports. It&apos;s the always-on AI analyst that hotel leaders have wanted and never had, until now.</p><p>In this episode, you&apos;ll hear:</p><ul><li>Why you can&apos;t trust most AI outputs yet, and what it takes to fix that</li><li>The CFO test: Would you hand this answer over and say, &quot;I know all of this is right&quot;?</li><li>Why your data has to be normalized, and the apples-to-apples problem</li><li>The questions every leader should ask their technical team about AI reporting</li><li>Introducing Altitude and the problem it was built to solve</li><li>A conversation with your data: following the thread without losing the plot</li><li>Why above-property leaders have been underserved, and why that ends here</li><li>The data pyramid: spending less time finding answers and more time acting on them</li></ul><p><a href='https://actabl.com/news/actabl-introduces-altitude/'>Learn more about Actabl Altitude here</a>.</p><p>Listen to prior episodes in this series:</p><ul><li><a href='https://podcast.hospitalitydaily.com/ai-layers/'>AI Only Works for Hotels in This Order: Data, Intelligence, Action - Stephen German, Actabl</a></li><li><a href='https://podcast.hospitalitydaily.com/actabl-hotel-data/'>Why Our Approach to Hotel Data Earned a Patent and Prepares Hotels for AI - Clark Brayton, Joseph McGroarty &amp; Pritesh Patel, Actabl</a></li><li><a href='https://podcast.hospitalitydaily.com/actabl-ai-asset-setup/'>Is Your AI Saving You Time?     <description><![CDATA[<p>In this episode,<a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/christopher-mcdowell-cissp-crisc-53890130/'> Chris McDowell</a>, Chief Information Security Officer at Actabl, and <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/justincall/'>Justin Call</a>, Chief Legal Officer at <a href='https://actabl.com/'>Actabl</a>, unpack the growing AI risk that many hotel leaders still underestimate. They explain how people across hospitality are already uploading confidential guest and operational data into public AI tools like ChatGPT and Claude, why privacy and compliance rules still apply once that data enters an LLM, and why AI adoption needs to be treated as a business risk decision, not just a productivity upgrade.<br/><br/>Chris and Justin also break down what strong AI governance actually looks like inside a hotel organization, how technical guardrails matter more than policy documents alone, and why the quality and normalization of your data will determine whether AI delivers meaningful value at scale.     <itunes:title>Why Work in Hospitality - Burak Ipekci</itunes:title>
    <title>Why Work in Hospitality - Burak Ipekci</title>

    <description><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/philippa-wagner-00820a23/'>Philippa Wagner,</a> the Founder of <a href='https://www.peopleplacesspaces.co.uk/'>People Places Spaces</a>, shares an honest perspective on AI and tech in hospitality, drawing on her <a href='https://www.peopleplacesspaces.co.uk/report'>Future Guest</a> research.     <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/philippa-wagner-00820a23/'>Philippa Wagner,</a> the Founder of <a href='https://www.peopleplacesspaces.co.uk/'>People Places Spaces</a>, shares an honest perspective on AI and tech in hospitality, drawing on her <a href='https://www.peopleplacesspaces.co.uk/report'>Future Guest</a> research.     <itunes:title>What Designer Hotels Miss About Real Hospitality - Tom Michelberger &amp; Matthias Huettebraeuker</itunes:title>

    <itunes:summary><![CDATA[In this episode, Tom Michelberger, founder of Michelberger Hotel in Berlin and Michelberger Farm in Brandenburg, shares with our innovation correspondent Matthias Huettebraeuker why depth beats scale, why most designer hotels lose their edge after a few years, and what it takes to integrate into a community without performing for it.     <description><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/tom-michelberger-a35729ab/'>Tom Michelberger</a>, founder of <a href='https://www.michelbergerhotel.com'>Michelberger Hotel</a> in Berlin and <a href='https://michelberger.farm/en'>Michelberger Farm</a> in Brandenburg, shares with our innovation correspondent <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/huettebraeuker/'>Matthias Huettebraeuker</a> why depth beats scale, why most designer hotels lose their edge after a few years, and what it takes to integrate into a community without performing for it. After 17 years and one hotel, Tom resists the word &quot;concept,&quot; refuses to scale, and treats hospitality as a conversation rather than a monologue.  </p><p>Featured in this conversation:</p><ul><li><a href='https://www.michelbergerhotel.com'>Michelberger Hotel</a> in Friedrichshain, Berlin</li><li><a href='https://michelberger.farm/en'>Michelberger Farm</a> in the     <description><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/peterriccihospitality/'>Dr. Peter Ricci</a>, Director of the Hospitality and Tourism Management Program at Florida Atlantic University, shares a practical view of AI in hospitality.     <content:encoded><![CDATA[<p>In this episode, <a href='https://www.linkedin.com/in/peterriccihospitality/'>Dr. Peter Ricci</a>, Director of the Hospitality and Tourism Management Program at Florida Atlantic University, shares a practical view of AI in hospitality.
